227 
BHASMA 


Definition 

Powder of a substance obtained by calcination 
is called Bhasma. In this section, it is applied to 
the metals and minerals and animal products which 
are, by special processes, calcined in closed 
crucibles in pits and with cow dung cakes (puta). 


Method of preparation 
First stage (S odhana) 

Bhasmas are prepared from purified minerals, 
metals and marine. and animal products. In 
Ayurveda, the process of purifiction is called 
Sodhana. Chemical purification is different from 
medicinal purification. In chemical purification it 
is only elimination of foreign matters. In medicinal 
purification the objects aimed at are (a) elimination 
of harmful matter from the drug; (b) modification 
of undesirable physical properties of the drug; (c) 
conversion of some of the characteristics of the 
drugs; (d) the enhancement of the therapeutic 
action, thereby potentizing the drug. 

Sodhana is of two kinds (1) Samanya Sodhana 
which is applicable to a large number of metals or 
minerals, as heating the thin sheets of the metals 
and immersing them in taila, takra, gomutra etc. 
(2) Visesa Sodhana which is applicable only to 
certain drugs and in certain preparations. ViSesa 
` 80078118 consists of (1) bhavania (2) Svedana (3) 
nirvapana, (4) mardana. 


Second stage (Marana) 

The second stage is the preparation of Bhasma. 
The purified drug is put into a Khalva (stone mortar 
and pestle) and ground with juices of the specified 
plants or kasayas of drugs mentioned for a 
particular mineral or metal. It is ground for the 
specified period of time. Then small cakes 
(cakrikas) are made. The size and thickness of the 
cakes depend on the heaviness of the drug, The 
heavier the drug, the thinner are the cakes. These 
cakes are dried well under sunlight and placed in 
one single layer in a shallow earthen plate (Sarava) 
and closed with another plate. The edge is sealed 


with clay-smeared cloth in seven consecutive 


layers and dried. 
A pit is dug in an open place. The diameter 
and the depth of the pit depends on the metal or 


mineral that is to be calcined. Half of the pit is 
filled with cow dung cakes. The sealed earthen 
container is placed in it and the remaining space 
is filled with more cow dung cakes. Fire is put on 
all four sides and in the middle of the pit. When 
the burning is over, it is allowed to cool itself 
completely. The earthen container is removed, 
the seal is opened and the contents are taken out. 
The medicine is ground into a fine powder in a 
khalva. This process of triturating with the juice, 
making cakrikas and giving putas, is repeated as 
many times as prescribed in the texts or till the 
proper fineness and quality are obtained. 

The putas are described under different names 
to indicate the size of the pit and the number of 
cow dung cakes to be used, details of which are 
given in the paribhasa. They also indicate the 
amount of heat required and the period of burning. 
The following putas are commonly used in the 
preparation of Bhasmas:- 

1. Maha puta 
Gaja puta 
Varaha puta 
Kukkuta puta 
Kapota puta 
Bhanda puta 


Characteristics and preservation 

The tests for properly prepared Bhasma are 
(1) there should be no candrika (metallic lustre) 
(nigcandrika) (2) when taken between the index 
finger and thumb and spread, it should be so fine 
as to get easily into the finger lines (rekha purita); 
(3) when a small quantity is spread on cold and 
still water, it should float on the surface (Varitaram); 
and (4) the bhasma should not revert to the original 
state(apunarbhava). 

Bhasmas, unless otherwise specified in 
individual formulations, are generally yellowish, 
black, pure white, grey, reddish black or red; 
depending upon the predominant drug as well as 
the other drugs used in the process of marana. 

Bhasmas are preserved in air tight glass or 
earthen containers. They maintain their potency 
indefinitely. They have no characteristic taste.

=] 8:1 ABHRAKA BHASMA 


Acceptable Variety 
(AyurvedaprakaSa,Adhyaya 2; 97-98.) 


शोधन 
aa तु वजवत्तिष्ठेन्नन चाग्नौ विकृतिं व्रजेत्‌ 
सर्वाभ्रेषु वरं वज्रं व्याधिवार्धक्यमृत्युजित्‌ 1197 || 
यदजञ्जननिभं क्षिप्तं न dal विकृति व्रजेत्‌ । 
वजसंज्ञं हिं तद्योग्यमभ्र सर्वत्र नेतरत्‌ 1198 || 
(आयुर्वेदप्रकाश, अध्याय 2, 97-98) 
sodhana 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 10:20.) 
शोधन 
अभ्रक वह्निसन्तप्तं सप्तवार निषेचितम्‌ 
गोदुग्धे वा वराक्वाथे शुद्धिमायात्यनुत्तमाम्‌ 1120 1 
(रसतरगिणी, AT 10.20.) 
1. Vajrabhraka (abhraka) QS. 
Godugdha 
or 
Vara (triphala) kvatha (P.) QS. (for 
nirvapana) 


Method of sodhana 

The vajrabhraka is heated to red hot in an iron 
pan and put in godugdha or triphala kvatha. This 
process is repeated seven times. Thereafter, it is 
pulverized for further processing. 


dhanyabhra 
(Ayurvedaprakasa, Adhyaya 2; 113-114%.) 
धान्याभ्र 
पादांशशालिसयुक्तमभर बद्‌ध्वाऽथ कम्बले 
त्रिरात्र स्थापयेन्नीरे क्लिन्नं वै मर्दयेत्करैः ।1113।| 
तन्नीर एव यत्नेन यावत्सर्व स्वेत्ततः । 
कम्बलाद्रलितं सूक्ष्म वालुका सदृशं च यत्‌ ।1114 ii 
तद्धान्याभ्रकमित्युक्तं मारणं चाप्यथो भवेत्‌ || 
(आयुर्वेद प्रकाश, अध्याय 2, 113-1 144) 
lig Abhra(abhraka)-suddha 1 part 
2. Sali (dhanya) (Fr.) 1/4 part 


3. Nira (kanjika) Q:S. 


Method of dhanyabhra 

The Sodhita abhraka is bundled in a jute or 
woollen bag with 1/4 quantity of paddy. This 
bundle is immersed in Kanjika for 3 days. There- 
after, the bundle is rubbed and squeezed in the 
liquid itself so that only abhraka particles escape 
through the fine holes of the bag. These particles 
are collected and dried. 


marana (a) 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 10; 39-42.) 


मारण (क) 
धान्याभ्रकं समादाय रविक्षीरैर्विमर्दयेत्‌ | 


रविमूलद्रवेर्वापि दिनमेक निरन्तरम्‌ 113 9 || 
ततः संशोषयेदघर्मे गगन कृतचक्रिकम्‌ 
रविपत्रेश्च संवेष्ट्य सप्तवारं विधानतः 114011 
पुटेद्गजयपुटे Aq रसतन्त्रविशारदः | 
ततो न्यग्रोधमूलोत्थेः क्वाथेर्दद्यात्पुटत्रयम्‌ 1141 11 
तद्दद्रम्भारसेनापि सप्तवार पुटेद्भिषक्‌ 
एव स्वल्पः पुटैरेव गगन मृतिमाप्नुयात्‌ 14211 
(रसतरगिणी, AT 10 3 9-42 ) 
1. Dhanyabhraka (abhraka) Q:S. 
2. Ravi ksira (arka) (L) Q.S. (for 
mardana) 
or 
Ravi mula rasa (arka) (Rt.) 
or

3. Nyagrodhamitla 
kvatha (Rt.) Q.S. (for 
mardana) 
4. Rambha (kadali) rasa (Rz.) Q:S. (for 
mardana) 


Method of marana (a) 

The dhanyabhra is ground well in ingredient 
No.2 for one day. Thin and flat cakrikas are made 
and dried under the sun. These cakrikas are kept 
in a Sarava samputa and sandhi lepa is done. There- 
after gaja puta is given. This process is to be re- 


231 


peated seven times. Then the abhraka is ground 
well in ingredients No.3, dried and gajaputa is 
given. This process is repeated three times. Again 
the abhraka is ground in ingredient No.4 and seven 
gaja putas are given saparately. In the end this 
should be ground in vata jata or vafankura kvatha 
and 3 gaja putas are given.

Method of marana (Sataputi vidhana) (b) 

The dhanyabhra is ground with ravi ksira for 
a day and flat thin cakrikas are prepared. Cakrikas 
are dried well under the sun and kept in Sarava 
samputa and sandhi lepa is done. Gaja puta is 
given. This process is repeated 10 times. Thereaf- 
ter abhraka is ground in other prescribed 9 liquids 
one by one and 10 gajaputas are given with each 
grinding. Thus, after completion of 100 putas it is 
ground well and preserved in stoppered glass or 
earthern containers.

= of marana (sahasraputi vidhana) (€) : 
The dhanyabhra is given bhavana with arka 
ksira for a day and flat, thin cakrikas are prepared. 
Cakrikas are dried well under sun and kept in 
Sarava samputa and sandhi lepa is done. Gajaputa 
is given. This process is repeated 17 times. There- 
after, abhraka is given bhavana in other prescribed 
59 liquids one by one and 17 gajaputas are given 
with each bhavana. Thus, after completion of 1000 
putas it is ground well and preserved in stoppered 
glass or porcelain containers. 
Dosage : 

125 mg. to 375 mg. 

Anupana : 

honey, ghee, triphala kvatha, gudiici svarasa, 
ardraka svarasa. 

Important therapeutic use : 
agnimandya; grahani; kapha roga; Svasa; jvara; 
rakta pitta; kasa; prameha; pandu; keSapatana; 
tvakroga; jara; napunsakata; kustha; pliha; 
udara; granthi; visa; krmi. 
used also as rasayana.

1. Kasisa 9.1 
2. Nimbu nira (nimbu) (Fr.)  Q.S. (for

Method of marana 

The Sodhita kasisa is ground with nimbu rasa 
and cakrikas are prepared and dried. These are 
kept in Sarava samputa and sandhi lepa is done. 
This is subjected to puta, filled with 7 kg. and 680 
grams of vanopala (cow dung). The puta is re- 
peated till the bhasma bright red (gairika varna) 
and sour taste is removed. This is generally got in 
3 or 4 putas. 
Dosage : 

250 mg. 
Anupana : 

bakuci curna, triphala kvatha, madhu. 
Important therapeutic use : 

pandu; pliharoga; hikka; vrana; visarpa; 31118; 

kastarttava; visa; daurbalya; netraroga.

1. Godanta (godanti) suddha Q:S. 


Method of miarana 

The Sodhita godanti is placed in Sarava samputa 
and subjected to gaja puta. The colour of the 
bhasma will be white. In some parts of the coun-

Method of visesa sodhana 

Saindhava is mixed with snuhi ksira and arka 
ksira to a paste which is smeared on tamra patra 
and allowed to dry. Thereafter, tamra patra is heated 
and immersed in nirgundi svarasa. This process is 
repeated 3 times. Another process is that tamra 
patra is heated and while on the oven snuhi ksira 
and arka ksira are dropped over it in small quanti- 
ties and allowed to evaporate.

Method of marana 

The thin patras of Sodhita tamra are boiled in 
nimbu rasa for 3 days. One fourth quantity of 
parada by weight is mixed with tamra patra and 
ground well for 3 hours adding nimbii rasa. 
Gandhaka, twice the quantity of tamra patra is 
ground with nimbt rasa and made into a bolus 
(golakam). To this golaka, kalka of minaksi, 
cangerl or punarnava is applied. The thickness of 
the lepa should be 3.75 cm. This is dried and: the 
golaka is kept at the bottom of a bhanda (pot). 
This is covered by a Sarava and sandhi is sealed 
with clay smeared cloth. The remaining upper 
portion of the pot is filled with sand. The mouth 
of the pot is covered with Sarava and sandhi is 
sealed. This pot is then placed on a oven and 
heated with kramavrddhi agni for 12 hours. When 
cooled the golaka is to be removed from the pot. 
This is ground with the juice of stirana kanda for 
one day. Gandhaka with little ghee is pasted over 
it and placed in a Sarava samputa and after sandhi 
lepa, is put to gaja puta three times.

Method of jarana (ahiphena) 

The sodhita trivanga (naga, vanga and yaSada 
in equal quantities) is kept in an iron pan and 
heated. While it is melting, powders of bhanga 
and ahiphena posta are sprinkled in small 
quantities and stirred with loha darvi (iron spatula). 
This process is continued till the melted trivanga 
is reduced to powder form.

Method of marana 

The jarita trivanga is ground in kumari svarasa 
for three hours. Flat cakrikas are prepared and dried 
well. These cakrikas are kept in Sarava samputa 
and sandhi bandha is made and placed in ardha 
gajaputa for calcination. This process is repeated 
seven times. The colour of bhasma will be pale 
yellow.

Method of Sodhana 

The naga is melted in an iron pan. It is poured 
into an iron pot containing taila, etc. in sufficient 
quantity. The pot should be covered with a heavy 
stone having a hole in the centre and the melted 
naga is poured through this hole. This process is 
to be repeated 3 times in each of the five drugs.

Method of marana (a) 
An amalgam of rajata patra and parada with 
nimbu svarasa is prepared. The nmbi is washed 


away with water. Equal part of gandhaka is added 
and ground well, then haratala is added, and 
ground weil. Small thin cakrikas are prepared. 
These are subjected to kukkuta puta seven times 
and thereafter laghuputa three times. 


marana (b) (Second method) 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 16; 37-40.) 
मारण (ख) 
समाहरेत्सुविमलं तारपत्रकणाच्वयम्‌

अध स्थं सुमृतं तार कृष्णाभमतिसुन्दरम्‌ 139 || 
आहरेदूर्ध्वसस्थन्तु रसेशञ्चातिनिर्मलम्‌ | 
एव मृतं चन्द्रलोहं तत्वाद्योगेषु योजयेत्‌ 114.0 11 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 16, 37-40) 
1. Tara patra kana(rajata)-suddha 1 part 
2. Hingula (drava)-suddha 2 parts 
3. Nimbtka (nimbt)-svarasa Q.:S. (for 
mardana) 


Method of marana (b) 

The 50411118 hingula (processed in nimbu 
svarasa) is to be washed with water and mixed 
particles of rajata and ground well. This is to be 
kept in Urdhva patana yantra and heated for 6 
hours. When cooled the pot is opened, and parada 
from the upper vessel and rajata from the lower 
vessel are collected. This process is repeated till 
the formation of proper bhasma. 

Dosage : 

125 mg. 
Anupana : 

citraka mila kasaya, parpataka kasaya, uSira 

kasaya, brahmi rasa, honey. 
Important therapeutic use : 

sosa; dhatuksaya; prameha; madatyaya; visa; 

jvara; pitta roga; pliha roga; buddhimandya; 

garbhasaya dosa; apasmara. 


Note :In 11518118 (a) three putas are 
prescribed and in marana (b) however 
as many as fourteen kukkuta putas are 


given traditionally.

Loha curna processed with sthali paka is 
washed with water and mixed with triphala kvatha 
and ground well. Thin flat cakrikas are prepared,

process is repeated 60 times.!

1. Patrakhya tala (haritala)! Suddha 1 part 
2. Punarnava-rasa (Pl.) QS. (for 
(rakta punarnava) mardana) 
3. Punarnava-ksara (?1.) Q:S. (for 
(rakta punarnava) yantrapurana) 


Method of miarana. 

The Sodhita haratala is ground well in 
punarnava svarasa for seven days and one cakrika 
of the entire quantity is prepared. The cakrika is 
dried. Half of punarnava ksara is filled in a copper 
sthali and the cakrika is placed over it. The other 
half of the punarnava 15818 is placed over the 
cakrika. Another Sthali is placed and the edge is 
sealed well. This is to be placed over an oven and 
heated with kramavrddha agni for 5 days 
continuously. 

Dosage : 

3144 to 125 mg. 
Anupana : 

honey, butter. 

Pathya : 

sastyodana, cow milk. 
Important therapeutic use : 

udara, pandu; vata roga; nadivrana; apasmara; 

rakta vikara; vatarakta; visarpa; kustha; arsa; 

bhagandara; rajayaksma; Svitra; visphota; 
dadru; pama; vicarcika. 





1. One important process in various methods of marana of haratala is that the tala cakrika is to be packed in 
tamra katorika. After sandhi lepa the tamra katorika is to be placed in the midst of punarnava ksara on 
apamarga bhasma. Then, this is to be heated for three to five days otherwise tala may loose much weight or 


may escape totally.

251 
MANDURA 


Definition 
These are preparations containing Sodhita 
mandura along with other drugs. 

Method of preparation 
Mandira is purified by a special method (the 
text is given under the first preparation) and 
boiled in gomiutra till it becomes a rasa-kriya 
(a paste). Then the powders mentioned in the 


yogas are added and stirred well. While warm, 

vatakas are prepared. 

This can be kept in powder form also. 
Characteristics and preservation 

These emit a strong smell of gomitra and are 

dark in colour. These preserve their potency 

indefinitely. These should be kept away from 

moisture.

255 
RASAYOGA 


Definiton 


Preparations containing mineral drugs as 
main ingredients are called Rasa Yogas. They may 
be in pill form. They are mixed and triturated to- 
gether. 


Method of preparation 


Drugs such as abhraka, maksika, svarna, 
rajata, tamra, kamsya etc. are used only in bhasma 
form in these preparations. Drugs such as 
gandhaka, manahsila etc. are used in purified 


form. Where rasa and gandhaka are drugs, kajjali 
is prepared first with these two and then only other 
drugs are added in small quantities and ground in 
the khalva itself and mixed well. 


Bhavana with the prescribed svarasa, kvatha 
etc. should be given to this for a prescribed pe- 
riod. 


Charateristics and preservation 

The colour and smell depend on the drugs 
in the yogas. They keep their potency indifinitely, 
unless otherwise prescribed.

20:6 EKANGAVIRA RASA 

(Rasarajasundara, page-458.) 
शुद्धं गधं मृतं सूतं कान्तं वंगं सनागकम्‌। 
ताम्र चाभ्र मृतं तीक्ष्णं नागर मरिचकणा।।

Special method of preparation : 


Kajjali of parada and gandhaka is prepared 
first. Drugs 3 to 27 should be added to it and 
mixed well. Bhavana each 7 times with drugs 
28 to 55 is given separately. The mass is made 
into a ball and dried. This ball is buried in a 


Lavana yantra and heated for a day continu- 
ously. When cooled, it is ground with drugs 
28 to 55 each seven times. In the end Kastiri 
and Karpura are added and when dried col- 
lected and kept in a bottle. 

Dosage : 
125 mg. 

Anupana : 
pippali ciirna, honey, gidiici sattva, butter.

SUla; grahani; vatodara; pandu; halimaka;

apasmara; Svasa; vatarakta; Sotha; adhamana; 
aruci; agnimandya; kasa; udara; dhatuSosa. 
given as rasayana and vajikarana also.

20:33 RASAMANIKYA 
(Siddhabhaisajyamanimala, Jvaraprakarana; 66.) 


अभ्रसम्पुटगं तालं किञ्चिदगारसाधितम्‌ | 
वातश्लेष्मज्वरे शस्तं माणिक्यरसशब्दितम्‌ ।1 66 ॥ 
(सिद्धभेषज्यमणिमाला, ज्वरप्रकरण; 66) 


1. Tala-Ssuddha (haratala) Q.S 

Special method of preparation : 
Small pieces of tala (haratala) are kept on 
abhraka(mica) sheet and covered by another 
sheet of abhraka. This abhraka samputa con- 
taining talaka is kept on moderate fire. When 
talaka is melted and colour is also changed 
like manikya (red) the samputa is removed 
from fire. When it is cool the abhraka sheets 
are separated and material found between 
sheets to be powdered and kept in a glass con- 
tainer. 

Dosage : 
125 to 250 mg. 

Anupana : 
ghee, honey. 

Important therapeutic use : 
vataslesma jvara; Svasa.

Special method of preparation : 


Kajjali of parada and gandhaka is prepared first 
and other drugs are added to it and bhavana 7 
times with jambira rasa is given. Thereafter the 
whole bolus is kept in a Sarava samputa and 
paka in baluka yantra for 24 hours is done. 
The drug should be removed from Sarava 
samputa and when cooled bhavana with drugs 
19 to 27 seven times each is given separately. 
After drying it is powdered. It is stored in a 
well stoppered bottle.

Definition Characteristics and Preservation 
Lauha kalpas are preparations of Lauha The powder should be very fine and the 
Bhasma as main ingredient added to other drugs. | bhasma used should be well prepared. When well 
protected from moisture and heat, they keep their 
Method of Preparation potency for a period of two years. Preparations 
The drugs are reduced to fine powder and | containing mercury or its compounds keep their 
mixed with lauha bhasma. Bhavana is given with | potency indefinitely.

1. SAMANYA PARIBHASA 
1. सामान्य परिभाषा 
ANUPANA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 6; 200.) 


अनुपान 
तत्तद्रोगघ्नभेषज्यं भेषजस्यानुपीयते 
यच्च साहाय्‌यकारि स्यादनुपान तदुच्यते 11200 || 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 6; 200) 

Anup4ana is an adjunct administered either 
along with or just after the principal medicine to 
enhance its therapeutic action. (When 
administered with the principal medicine it is 
described as Sahapana, though in practice, the term 
anupana connotes both .) 


AMRTIKARANA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 2; 58.) 
अमृतीकरण 

लोहादीनां Fart वै शिष्टदोषापनुत्तये । 
क्रियते यस्तु संस्कार अमृतीकरणं मतम्‌ 115 8 || 
(रसतरगिणी, AT 2; 58) 
Amrtikarana is a process adopted to remove 


the residual dosas and to enhance the therapeutic 
action of drugs, as in the case of tamra bhasma. 


KAJJALI 

(Rasatarangini, Taranga 2; 27.) 
कज्जली 
निर््रवेधतुभिश्चाथ गन्धादिभिः | 
पेषितः पारदः श्लक्ष्णतां प्रापितः 
कज्जलाभो यदा जायतेऽसौ तदा 

नामतः कोविदैः कज्जलीत्युच्यते 

(रसतरगिणी, तरग 2; 27) 


| 56 


When sulphur (gandhaka) is added to Mercury 
(parada) and triturated without adding any liquid 
till it becomes a very fine black powder, it is called 
Kajyjali. 


KUPIPAKA 
Kupipaka is a process by which, formulations 
are prepared in a kaca kupi which is heated as 
directed. 


KANJIKA _ 
(Synonym : Dhanyamla, Aranala) 
(Paribhasapradipa) 


काञ्जिक 
अन्न शाल्यादिससिद्धं प्रक्षिप्तं त्रिगुणे जले । 
धान्याम्लं सन्धितम्‌ प्रोक्तमारनालं च काञ्जिकम्‌| 
शालिकोद्रवमण्डेर्वा सन्धितं काञ्जिकम्‌ भवेत्‌ ।। 
(परिभाषा प्रबध) 


Powder of ASudhanya such as Kulmasa, 
Sasthika rice, etc., along with small quantity of 
white radish (miulaka), cut into pieces, 768 g. are 
placed in an earthern pot and 3.72 litres of water 
is added. The mouth of the pot is closed and kept 
for two to three weeks during which period the 
fluid becomes sour. This sour fluid is called 
Kanjika, Dhanyamla or Aranala. 


GHANA 


The drug is taken in a vessel and eight or 
sixteen times of water is added and boiled on mild 
fire until one-eighth liquid is remained. It is then 
filtered and boiled again until water content is 
evaporated to the maximum. 


GHANA SATTVA 

The dravya of which Ghana Sattva is to be 
prepared is first washed well with water and cut 
into small pieces and made into Javkiit and put in 
a vessel. Four times water is added in that vessel 
and boiled on mild fire till one-fourth water 
remained. It is then filtered. The decoction is 
further heated till the water is evaporated and the 
decoction is reduced to a semisolid substance. It 
could be further dried under sun and made into 
pill form. 


CANAKAMLA 


During winter months the dew drops appearing 
in the morning on the herbs of Cana plant (Cicer 
arientinum) are collected. This collection can be 
done by (1) scrapping directly from the plant, (11) 
touching the plant with palms and scrapping the 
material that stick to palms or (ili) covering the 
plants with a thin cloth in the night and the cloth 
is removed in the morning and washed well in 
water kept in a stainless steel vessel. Later this ` 
water is evaporated by heating until a very small 
amount is remained. Later this liquid is further 
dried by keeping the vessel under sun. This product 
is called as Canakamla or Canaksara. 


349 


CURNODAKA/SUDHODAKA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 11; 216-217.) 
चूर्णोदक / सुघोदक 
Vega चूर्ण पञ्चतोलकसंमिते 


जले विनिक्षिपेत्पराज्ञस्त्रियामं स्थापयेद्‌ बुधः 11216 I 

ततः सारक पत्रेण सारयेत्काचपात्रके | 

चर्णोदकमिति ख्यातं तथेव च सुधोदकम्‌ 11217 || 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 11; 216-217) 


The filtrate obtained by mixing 60 ml. of water 
with 250 mg. of lime powder, keeping for 9 hours 
and then filtering through a filter paper after 
decantation, is called Cirnodaka or Sudhodaka. 


DHALANA 
(Rasaratnasamucchaya, Adhyaya 8; 43.) 


ढालन 
दरुतलोहस्य निक्षेपो द्रवे तद्‌ ढलनं मतम्‌ 
(रसरत्नसमुच्चय, अध्याय 8; 43) 


|| 4311 


Dhalana is the process of pouring molten 
metals into the liquid specified. 


TAKRA 
(Susrutasamhita, Sitrasthana, Adhyaya 45; 85.) 
| तक्र 
मन्थनादिपृथग्भूतस्नेहमर्धोदक च यत्‌ 
नाति सान्द्रद्रवं तक्र स्वाद्वम्लं तुवरं रसे 118 5 || 
(सुश्रुतसंहिता, सूत्रस्थान, अध्याय 45; 85) 


Takra is the liquid obtained by adding equal 
quantity of water to curd (dadhi) and decanting 
the same by churning. 


TUSAMBU 
(Sarngadharasamhita, Madhyamakhanda, 
Adhyaya 10;11.) 
तुषाम्बु 

तुषाम्बुसन्धितं ज्ञेयमामेर्विदलिते्यविः || 

(शां.धरसंहिता, मध्यम खण्ड, अध्याय 10; 11) 
Tusambu is the decanted liquid obtained from 

a mixture of equal quantity of crushed barley and 


warm water kept over night. 


TANDULODAKA 
(Sarigadharasamhita, Madhyama khanda, 
Adhyaya 1; 29.) 
तण्डुलोदक 
खण्डितं तण्डूलपलं धु णिते क्षिपेत्‌ । 
भावयित्वा जलं ग्राह्यं देयं कर्मसु 1129 || 
(शां ङ.धर संहिता, मध्यम खण्ड, अध्याय 1; 29) 


Tandulodaka is the liquid obtained by mixing 
48 g. of rice with 385 ml. of water keeping for 
two hours and thereafter decanting. 


DADHI 
(Susruta Samhita, Siitrasthana, Adhyadya 45; 77) 
दधि | 
शृतातक्षीरात्तु यज्जातं गुणवदधि तत्‌ स्मृतम्‌ 1177 | 
(सुश्रुत सहिता सूत्रस्थान, अध्याय 45, 77) 
Dadhi is curd. 


DHUPANA 
Dhipana means the process of fumigating a 
pot or a vessel with the prescribed drugs. 


NIRUTTHA/APUNARBHAVA 
(Dravyagunavijnana, Paribhasakhanda) 
! Freea/ aqada: 
गुड Pot सुखस्पर्श-मध्वाज्यैः सह योजितम्‌। 


नायाति प्रकृति ध्मानादपुनर्भवमुच्यते 13 8 || 
रोप्येणं सहसयुक्तं ध्यातं रोप्येण नो लगेत । 
तदा निरूत्थमित्युक्तम्‌ लौह तदपुनर्भवम्‌ 1139 || 


(द्रव्यगुण विज्ञान, परिभाषा खण्ड, अध्याय 4; 38-39) 


Niruttha/Apunarbhava is the condition of 
bhasma from which it cannot be reverted to its 
metallic form even if mixed with mitrapancaka 
and heated to the temperature at which the bhasma 
is prepared. 


NIRMALIKARANA OF TANKANA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 13; 75-76) 


निर्मलीकरण 


cou चूर्णितं तोये crag भिषक्‌ 

सद्राव्य वस्त्रपूतञ्च कृत्वा yee विधापयेत्‌ ।। 75 | 

तीत्राग्निना पचेत्कामं नीर च परिशोषयेत्‌ 

स्वल्पनीराशशेषे च टकणं निर्मलं हरेत्‌ || 76 I 
(रसतरगिणी, AT 13;75-76) 


One part of tankana is added to 24 parts of 
water and dissolved. It is filtered and the filtrate is 


350 


boiled to evaporate the water and dried to remove 
moisture completely. 


NIRVAPANA/NIMAJJANA 
(Rasendracidamani, Adhyaya 4;77.) 


Nirvapana/Nimajjana is the process of 
immersing the heated solid ingredients in a liquid 


specified. 
PARPATI 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 2; 42.) 
पर्पटी 


Waren कज्जलिकाग्नियोगात्‌ रम्भापलाशे चिपिटीकृता 

च ६ 

रसागमज्ञैः खलु पर्पटी सा प्रकीर्तिता पर्पटिका च सेव 114 2 1 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 2; 42) 


Parpati is a thin flake prepared out of melted 
kajjali. 


PANAKA 
(Dravyagunavigyan, 1/30) 


पानक 
फलमम्लमनम्लं वा wary परिमर्दितम्‌ 
सितामरिचसयुक्तं पूतं स्यात्‌ पानक WAI! 
(द्रव्यगुणविज्ञान, 1/3 0) 


Like Amra, Dadima, Pariisaka, Cinca, Draksa 
pinda kharjiira etc. which are half ripended, Amla 
and Anamla (Madhura) fruits are taken and added 
16 times of water. It is soaked over night and 
rubbed, so as to extract the juice and filtered with 
cloth. Sita and Marica are added in required 
quantities. Sometimes Ela, Lavanga and Kesara 
are added to impart fragrance to Panaka. 


PRAKSEPA 


Praksepa is the fine powder of drugs added to 
a kalpa such as leha, asavarista etc. 


PRATINIDHI DRAVYA 
Pratnidhi dravya is a substitute drug as 
described in Ayurvedic classical texts to be used 
only when the original drug is not available. No 
substitute is permissible in the case of principal 
drug in the formula. 


BHANUPAKA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 20; 21.) 


भानुपाक 
वराक्वाथयुतं लौह भानोः प्रखरभानुभिः | 
शुष्यन्‌ विपच्यते यस्माद्‌ भानुपाकस्ततः स्मृतः ।121 || 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 20.21) 


Bhanupaka is the process of evaporating the 
moisture of kvatha by exposure to sun. 


BHAVANA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 2; 49.) 
भावना 
यच्चूर्णितस्य धात्वादेरद्रवैः सम्पेष्य शोषणम्‌ । 
भावन तन्मतं विज्ञेभीवना च निगद्यते 149 11 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 2; 49) 
Bhavana is the process by which powders of 


drugs are ground to a soft mass with liquid 
substances and allowed to dry. 


MARDANA 
Mardana is the process of trituration of drugs 
to a fine state of division with or without prescribed 
liquid. 
MASTU 
(Paribhasaprabhandha) 
मस्तु 

दध्नो मण्डस्तु मस्त्विति | 

(परिभाषा प्रबन्ध) 


Mastu is the liquid separated from curd. 


MARANA 
(Paribhasaprabhanda) 


मारण 
शोधितां लौहधात्वादीन्‌ विमर्द्य स्वरसादिभिः | 
अग्निसियोगतो भस्मीकरण मारण स्मृतम्‌ || 
(परिभाषा प्रबन्ध) 
Marana is the process by which metals and 


minerals are ground with liquids (svarasa, etc.) 
and when dry reduced to bhasma by heat. 


MURCHANA 
(Bhaisajya Ratnavali, Jvaradhikara, 1283-84) 
मूर्छन 
आदो समूच्छयेत्‌ स्नेह क्वाथं संचारयेत्ततः। 
दुग्धं कल्क क्रमादद्यात्‌ गन्धद्रव्यं ततः TAI! 


351 


एवं रीत्या मन्दमन्दानलेनैव पचेद्‌भिषक्‌। 
निर्मलं निर्जलं दृष्ट्वा स्नेहसिद्धं तु लक्षयेत्‌।। 
(भेषज्यरत्नावली, ज्वाराधिकार, 1283-84) 


Before preparing any Sneha preparation (ghrta 
or taila) mirchana of the same has to be done. 
Mirchana removes amadosa of taila/ghrta and 
gives best colour and fragrance. Murchita 
Snehadravyas are to be boiled with kvatha dravyas 
and later with kalka dravyas. 


TAILA MURCHANA 
(Bhaisajya Ratnavali, Jvaradhikara, 1286-87) 
तैलमूरच्छन 


तेलं कृत्वा कटाहे दृदतरविमले मन्दमन्दानलैस्तत्‌ 
तेलं निष्फेन भावं गतमिह च यदा शेत्यभाव समेत्य | 
मज्जिष्ठारात्रिलोधेर्जलधरनलिकैः सामलैः साक्षपथ्यैः 
सूचीपुष्पाड्प्रिनीरेरुपहितकथितेर्गन्धयोगं जहाति || 
तेलस्येन्दुकलांशिकेकविकसा भागोऽपि मूरच्छविधौ 
ये चान्ये त्रिफलापयोदरजनीहीवेरलो धान्विताः। 
सूचीपुष्पवटावरोहनलिकास्तस्याश्च पादांशिका- 
दुर्गन्धं विनिहन्ति तेलमरुणं सौरभ्यमाकूर्वते।। 
(भेषज्यरत्नावली, ज्वराधिकार, 1286-87) 


Tila 19118 of 1.536 1. is taken in a vessel and

1. Water 6.1441. 
2. Manjistha (Rt.) 96 g.

4.  Bibhitaki (P.) 24 g. 
5. Amalaki (P.) 24 £.

12. Nalika (naluka) (St. Bk.) 24 g. 


Boiling continued till a small quantity of water 
remained and filtered. Mtrchana removes 
gandhadosa also of oil.

MUSA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 3; 7.)

RASAKRIYA/GHANA 
(Dalhana’s Commentary on Susruta Samhita,

रसक्रिया 
रसक्रिया कल्पना चेय ~ द्रव्यापेक्षयाऽष्टगुणं षोडशगुणं वा 
जलं दत्वाऽष्टभागावशेषः षोडशभागावशेषो वा क्वाथः कार्यः, 
ततः पूतं कषायं पुनस्तावत्‌ पचेद्यावत्‌ फाणिताकृतिः। 
(सुश्रुतसहिता, सूत्रस्थान, अध्याय 37/21 पर Seen)

(Sarngadharasamhita, Madhyamakhanda, 

Adhyaya 8/1.) 
क्वाथादीना YA: पाकात्‌ घनत्वं सा रसक्रिया | 
(शा ई.घरसंहिता, मध्यम खण्ड, अध्याय 8/1) 


The solidified state of kvatha etc., after it is 
again cooked/boiled, is called as rasakriya. 


LAKSARASA 
(Paribhasapradipa 2, 69.) 


लाक्षारस 
षडगुणेनाम्भसा लाक्षा दोलायन्त्रे ह्युपरिथ्ता | 
त्रिसप्तधा परिस्राव्या लाक्षारसमिद विदुः 16 9 || 
(परिभाषा प्रदीप, खण्ड 2, 69) 


Laksarasa is the liquid obtained by subjecting 
the bundled mass of laksa in six times of water in 
dolayantra and straining it, twenty one times. 


VAJRODANA 
(Rasaratnakar, Riddhikhanda 3; 48-49) 


वजोदन 
मातुलुग गतं da रुद्धुवा बाह्ये मृदा लिपेत्‌ । 
पचेत्‌ पुटे समुद्धृत्य तदवच्छतपुटेः पचेत्‌ 114.8 II 
नागवल्या द्रवेर्म् (लिप्त) तत्पत्रेणेव वेष्टितम्‌ | 
जानुमध्यस्थितं याम तदजं मृदुतां व्रजेत्‌ 49 || 
(रसरत्नाकर, ऋद्धिखड 3; 48-49) 
Vajra is kept inside the fruit of Bijora nimbu 
and covered with clay, dried and given 100 putas. 


Later it is pasted in a mortar with juice of betel 
leaf and covered with leaves of betel and put 
between Janu for 3 hours. 


SARAVA 
Sarava is a round shaped shallow earthen vessel. 


SODHANA 
Sodhana is the process of removal of impurities 
and potentisation of drugs. 


SURA AND PRASANNA 
(Paribhasaprabandha) 


सुरा ओर प्रसन्ना 
परिपक्वान्नसन्धानं समुत्पन्नां सुरा जगुः 
सुरामण्डः प्रसन्ना स्यात्‌ ततः कादम्बरी धना |! 
(परिभाषा प्रबन्ध) 


Sura is the supernatant liquid containing self 
generated alcohol obtained on subjecting to 
fermentation the mixture of rice or any other 
cooked cereal. Prasanna is the clear supernatant 
portion of sura. 


SANDHANA 
(Paribhasaprabhandha) 


सन्धान 

केवलं द्रवद्रव्यं वा भेषजान्नादि संयुतम्‌ 

चिरकालस्थितं वेद्ये: सन्धानं परिकीतितम्‌ ।। 
(परिभाषा Weer) 


Sandhana is the process of fermentation of 
liquids either alone or with drugs, by keeping them 
in a closed vessel for a specified time. 


SANDHI LEPANA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 3; 5) 


सन्धिलेपन 
मूषादीना तं यत्सन्धौ किट्टद्यैः स्याद्विलेपनम्‌ | 
तत्सन्धिलेपनं ख्यातं तच्चोक्तं सन्धिबन्धनम्‌ 115 II 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 3; 5) 


9910111 lepana is sealing the edges of a vessel, 
with the lid on, at the point of contact, with a paste 
of clay and cloth. 


353 


STHALIPAKA 
(Rasatarangini, Taraiga 20; 25.) 
स्थालीपाक 
त्रिफलाक्वथितोपेतं लौह स्थाल्यां खराग्निना । 
शुष्यन्‌ विपच्यते यस्मात्‌ स्थालीपाकस्ततः स्मृतः।125 | 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 20; 25) 


The paka of lauha made with triphala kvatha 
in a sthali (iron pan) on strong fire is called 
Sthalipaka. 


SVANGASITA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 2; 47.) 
स्वागशीत 
ज्वलनस्थितमेवेह शीतलत्वमुपेति यत्‌ | 
स्वागशीतं तदुदिदष्टं स्वतः शीतञ्च तन्मतम्‌ 147 || 
(रसतरगिणी, TT 2; 47) 
When heated materials attain atmospheric 

temperature in situ, it is called Svangasita. 


2. KALPANA PARIBHASA 
2. कल्पना परिभाषा 


KALKA 
(Paribhasaprabandha) 


कल्क 
यः पिण्डो रसपिष्टानां स कल्कः परिकीर्तितः । 
(परिभाषाप्रबन्ध) 


Kalka is the fine paste of macerated fresh plant 
material. 


KVATHA 
(Carakasamhita, Siitrasthana Adhyaya 4; 8.) 
क्वाथ 
dal तु क्वथितं द्रव्यं शृतमाहुश्चिकित्सकाः । 
(चरक संहिता, सूत्र स्थान; अध्याय 4; 8) 


(Dravyagunavijnana, Paribhasakhanda) 

Kvatha is the filtered decoction obtained by 
boiling coarse powder ग drug (s) in proportion of 
4 and 8 times for mrdu and madhyama dravyas 
respectively of water and reduced to one-fourth 
and 16 times of water for Kathina dravyas and 
reduced to one-eighth. 


CURNA 
(paribhasapradipa) 


i 
अत्यन्तशुष्क yaaa सुविष्ट वस्त्रगालितम्‌ । 
चूर्ण तच्च रजः क्षोदस्तस्य पर्याय उच्चते |11 5 || 
(परिभाषा प्रदीप, द्वितीय खण्ड, 15) 


The fine sieved powder of well-dried drug (5) 
is called (प्ता). 


PUTAPAKA SVARASA 
(Sarigadharasamhita, Madhyamakhanda, 
Adhyaya 1; 22-24%.) 
पुटपाक स्वरस 
पुटपाकस्य कल्कस्य स्वरसो गृह्यते यतः | 


अतस्तु पुटपाकानां युक्त्तिरत्रोच्यते मया 112 2 || 
पुटपाकस्य AAT लेपस्याङ्ग गरवर्णता 
लेपं च दयगुल स्थूलं कुरयद्वागुष्टमात्रकम्‌ 1123 || 
काश्मरी वटजम्ब्वादि पत्रर्वेष्टनमुत्तमम्‌ 
पलमात्रं रसो ग्राह्यः कर्षमात्र मधु क्षिपेत्‌ 112 4 || 


कल्कचूर्णद्रवाद्यास्तु देयाः स्वरसवद्‌बुधैः 
(शाङ्ग.धरसंहिता, मध्यम खण्ड, अध्याय 1, 
22-24%) 


Putapaka svarasa is the juice of green herb 
obtained by the process of putapaka. The Kalka 
of green plant material is bundled in leaves of 
gambhari, vata, jambu etc. The bundle is covered 
with clay in layers of about 2 cm. thickness. When 
the clay is dried, the bundle is placed amidst fire 
till it becomes reddish. The bundle is then opened 
and juice from Kalka is pressed out. 


PHANTA 
(Paribhasapradipa 2, 26.) 
फाण्ट 


क्षुण्णे द्रव्यपले सम्यग्जलमुष्णं विनिक्षिपेत्‌ | 
पात्रे चतुपलमितं ततस्तु स्रावयेज्जलम्‌ || 
सोऽयं पूतो द्रवः फाण्ट भिषग्भिरभिधीयते ।126 || 


(परिभाषा प्रदीप, दितीय खड 26) 


The drug is powdered first. Four times the 
quantity of boiling water is added to it and kept 
for sometime. When cooled, it is filtered and used. 
The Phanta is the infusion obtained by pouring 
four times of boiling water on the powdered drug 
(s) and strained when cooled. 


354 


SVARASA 
(Sarngadharasamhita, Madhyamakhanda, 
। Adhyaya 1; 2.) 
स्वरस 
अहतात्तत्क्षणाकृष्टाद्‌द्रव्यात्कषुण्णात्समुद्‌भवः । 
वस्त्र निष्पीडितो य: स रसः स्वरस उच्यते ।12 II 
(शां ह.धरसंहिता, मध्यम खंड, अध्याय 1; 2) 


The liquid juice of fresh macerated plant 
material obtained by pressing through a cloth is 
called Svarasa. 


HIMA KASAYA 
(Sarmgadharasamhita, Madhyamakhanda; 
Adhyaya 4; 1.) 


हिमकषाय 
क्षुण्णं द्रव्यपलं सम्यक्‌ षड्भिर्नीरपलैः प्लुतम्‌ । 
निशोषितं हिमः स स्यात्तथा शीतकषायकः ।।1 || 
(शां ईह .धरसंहिता, मध्यम खंड, अध्याय 4.1) 


The Hima kasaya is the extractive obtained on 
straining through cloth 24 g. of powdered drug(s) 
soaked in 144 ml. of water overnight. 


3. PUTA PARIBHASA 
3. पुट परिभाषा 
PUTA | 
(Rasantarangini, Taranga 3; 32.) 
पुट 
रसोपरसलोहादेः पाकमानप्रमापकम्‌ 
उत्पलाद्यग्निसियोगात्‌ यत्तदत्र पुट स्मृतम्‌ 113211 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 3; 32) 
The Puta is the measure of heating arrangement 


meant for preparing various kinds of Bhasma of 
maharasa, uparasa, loha etc. 


KAPOTAPUTA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 3; 43.) 
कपोतपुट 
वन्योत्पलैरष्टसख्येः क्षितौ यद्दीयते पुटम्‌ । 
रसादीनान्तु सिद्धयर्थ तत्कपोतपुट स्मृतम्‌ 1143 11 
(रसरतगिणी, AT 3; 43) 


The Kapotaputa is an arrangement using eight 
cow-dung cakes as fuel (vanyopala). 


GAJAPUTA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 3; 40) 
गजपुट 

नृपकरचतुरस्रोत्सेधदेर्घ्ये तु कुण्डे छगणगणभृता्दधमूषिकां 
स्थापयित्वा | 

पुटनमिह भवेद्यच्छणपूर्णेऽरदधभागेगजपुटमिहतन्त्रे भाषितं 
तद्रसज्ञैः 114011 

(रसतरगिणी, AT 3, 40) 


The Gajaputa is an arrangement of heating in 
a pit of 90 cms in length, breadth and depth. Half 
the pit is filled with cow-dung cakes. The 
Saravasamputa is put upon it, the empty space 
above is filled again with cow-dung cakes and 
ignited. 


VARAHAPUTA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga; 3, 41.) 
वाराहपुट 
कुण्डत्वरत्निमानेन चतुरस तथोच्छिते | 
पुट यद्‌ दीयते तत्तु वाराहपुटमुच्यते || 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 3, 41) 


The varahaputa is an arrangement of heating 
in a pit 60 cms in length, breadth and depth. Half 
the pit is filled with cow-dung cakes. The Sarava 
samputa is put upon it, and the empty space above 
is filled with cow-dung cakes and ignited. 


4. YANTRA PARIBHASA 
4. यत्र परिभाषा 


ARKAYANTRA 
(Synonyms-Mocikayantra, Mayirayantra) 
The Arkayantra is a distillation device with a 

condensor of double walled copper jacket. 


KHALVA YANTRA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 4; 53.) 
खल्वयत्र 
चषकोपममत्यच्छ सुदृढ पिच्छिलोपमम्‌ 
सच्छिलाविहितं पात्र खल्वयंत्रमिहोच्यते 1153 11 
(रसतरगिणी, AT 4, 53) 


Khalvayantra denotes mortar with pestle, 
made of various stones of good quality in different 
shapes and sizes. It is also made of metals . 


355 


a YANTRA 
(Synonyms : Vidyadhara yantra, Utthapana 
yantra, Adhah patana yantra) 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 4 : 41.) 
डमरुयत्र 
(विद्याधरयत्र, उत्थापन यत्र, ऊर्ध्वपातन य॑त्र) 
स्थालिकोपरि विन्यस्य स्थाली न्युब्जतयापराम्‌। 
पचेद्यथाक्रम त्वेतद्यन्त्र डमरुकाहवयम्‌ 1141 || 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 4; 41) 


Damaruyantra is a contravenes of shape 
resembling Damaru for sublimation prepared by 
sealing two pots with their mouths one telescoping 
the other sealing joint securely. 


TIRYAK PATANA YANTRA 
(AyurvedaprakaSa, Adhyaya 1; 79-81%.) 


तिर्यक पातन यत्र 
घटे रसं विनिक्षिप्य सजलं घटमन्यकम्‌ 


तिर्यड्मुख दयो कृत्वा समुखं रोधयेत्सुधीः = 79॥ 
चुल्ल्या तथेव संस्थाप्य यत्नतस्तु ततोभिषक्‌ | 
रसाधो ज्वालयेदग्नि AES विशेत्‌ ।। 80 ॥ 
तिर्यकपातनमित्युक्तं | 
मिश्रितो चेद्रसे नागवङ्गगै विक्रम हेतुना || stil 


ताभ्यां स्यात्कृत्रिमो दोषस्तन्मुक्तिः पातनत्रयात्‌ 
(आयुर्वेदप्रकाश, अध्याय 1, 79-81 %) 


Tiryak patana yatra is an iron retort prepared 
for distillation of Mercury with the delivery tank 
weld to it at an angle of 45°. 


DARVIKA YANTRA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 4; 39.) 
दर्विका यत्र 
चषक दर्विकाकल्पं वीर्घहस्तकसंयुतम्‌ | 
दर्विकायन्त्रमेतद्धि गन्धशोधनसाधकम्‌ 113.9 || 
(रसतरगिणी, AT 4; 39) 


An iron ladle, with a long handle is called 
Darvikayantra 


DOLA YANTRA 
(Rasaratnasamuccaya, Adhyaya 9; 3-4.) 


दोलायत्र 
द्रवद्रव्येण भाण्डस्य पूरितार्धोदकस्य च 


मुखस्योभयतो द्वारद्रय कृत्वा प्रयत्नतः 113 1 
तयोस्तु निक्षिपेदण्ड तन्मध्ये रसपोटलीम्‌ 
वद्ध्वा तु स्वेदयेदेतद्ोलायन्त्रमिति स्मृतम्‌ 14 II 


(रसरत्नसमुच्चवय, अध्याय 9; 3-4) 


Dolayantra is a contravene consisting of a pot 
half filled with liquid with a horizontal rod put on 
the rim from which is suspended the bundle of 
material to be heated. 


VALUKA YANTRA 
(Synonym : Lavana yantra) 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 4 : 29-31.) 
बालुकायत्र 
(पर्याय : लवणयत्र) 


सवस्त्रकुट्टितमृदा लिप्ताङ्गगेञ्च विशोषिताम्‌ । 


रसाविपूर्णजठरां काचकृषीन्तु विन्यसेत्‌ 112 9 || 
स्थाल्यां मृत्तललिप्तायां सुदृढायां प्रयत्नतः । 
आकण्ठं कूपिकां तत्र बालुकाभिः प्रपूरयेत्‌ 13 0 || 
भाण्डाधो ज्वालयेदग्निं यथाकालं यथाक्रमम्‌ | 
एतद्‌ बुधे: समाख्यातं बालुकायंत्र संज्ञकम्‌ 1131 I 


(रसतरगिणी, तरग 4; 29-31) 


Valukayantra is a sand bath heating 
contravenes where in a wide mouth round pot is 
one-fourth filled with sand and then the glass 


bottle containing the ingredients covered with 


seven layers of mudsmeared cloth is lowered to 
the bottom of the pot and empty space is filled 
with sand up to the neck of the bottle and then 
heated for specified time.

is ABHRAKA SODHANA 
Acceptable Variety 

(AyurvedaprakaSa, Adhyaya 2; 97-98.) 
ग्राहूयाभ्रक 

वजरं तु वजवत्तिष्ठेननन चाग्नौ विकृति ब्रजेत्‌ । 


सर्वाभ्रिषु वरं वज्रं व्याधिवार्धक्यमृत्युजित्‌ 1197 || 
यदजञ्जननिभं कषिप्तं न dal विकृति व्रजेत्‌ । 
वजसंज्ञ हिं तद्योग्यमभ्र सर्वत्र नेतरत्‌ 1198 11 


(आयुर्ेदप्रकाश, अध्याय 2, 97-98) 


sodhana 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 10:20.) 


शोधन 
अभ्रक वदह्िसन्तप्तं सप्तवार निषेचितम्‌ 


गोदुग्धे वा वराक्वाथे शुद्धिमायात्यनुत्तमाम्‌ 1120 1 
(रसतरगिणी, AT 10.20. 
1. Vajrabhraka (abhraka) QS. 
2. Godugdha 
or 


Vara (triphala) kvatha (P.) 


QS. (for 
nirvapana) 
Method of sodhana 

The vajrabhraka is heated to red hot in an iron 
pan and put in godugdha or triphala kvatha. This 
process is repeated seven times. Thereafter, it is 
pulverized for further processing.

7. KUPILU SODHANA 
(Rasamrta, Parisista 8; Page 147.) 


गवां मूत्रे कुपीलुं तु स्थापयेत्‌ सप्तरात्रकम्‌ । 
तत उद्धुत्य Mea दोलायन्त्रे विपाचयेत्‌ || 
याममात्रं ततः कृत्वा त्वगङ्कु.रविवर्जितम्‌ 
नीरेण क्षालयित्वाऽथ रसयोगेषु योजयेत्‌ || 
(रसामृत, परिशिष्ट 8; पृष्ठ 147) 


1. Kupilu (Sd.) 1 part 
2. Go-miutra Q:S. for 
sthapana 
3. Go-dugdha Q.S. for 
svedana 


Method 

Kupilu is kept in go-miutra for 7 days. Fresh 
go-mutra is to be replaced every day. Thereafter 
it is removed and washed with water. Svedana in 
go-dugdha with dola yantra for 3 hours is done. 
The testa and embryo are removed, the cotyledon 
is roasted in ghee and powdered well.

coated vessel and poured in bhrngaraja rasa.

14. GANDHAKA SODHANA 
(Rasamrta, Adhyaya 2; 3.) 


गव्ये पयति गन्धं तु भृगराजरसेऽथवा | 


रसपर्पटिकाप्रोक्तविधानेन विशोधयेत्‌ || 3 || 
(रसामृत, अध्याय 2, 3) 
. Gandhaka 1 part 
2. Go-dugdha Q:S. 
or 
Bhrngaraja-rasa (P1.) Q:S. 


Method 

Gandhaka melted in an iron pan smeared with 
ghee and is poured in a pot containing godugdha 
or bhrngaraja rasa and collected when cooled. It 
is washed, dried and then kept for use. 

For the purpose of Rasa Parpati preparation 3 
or 7 Bhavanas of Bhrifgaraja are given in 
powdered gandhaka. Then gandhaka is melted in 
Ghrt coated vessel and poured in Bhriigaraja rasa. 


15. GUGGULU SODHANA 
(1) Sand stone, glass etc. in Guggulu, if present, 
are first removed. 
(2) It is then broken into small pieces. 


364 


(3) It is thereafter bundled in a piece of the cloth 
and boiled in Dola Yantra containing any one 
of the following fluids. 

(a) Gomitra. 

(b) Triphalakasaya. 

(c) Vasapatra kasaya. 

(d) Vasapatra svarasa. 

(€) Nirgundipatra svarasa with Haridra ctirna; 
and 

(f) Dugdha. 


The boiling is continued till the Guggulu be- 
comes a soft mass. It is then taken out of the cloth 
and spread over a smooth wooden board smeared 
with ghee or oil. 


By pressing with fingers the sand and other 
remaining foreign impurities are removed. It is 
taken out and again fried with ghee and ground in 
a stone mortar (khalva). This is called Sodhita 
Guggulu. 


The other method is to suspend the bundle of 
Guggulu in Dola Yantra so as to remain immersed 
in the specified fluid as it is boiled. The boiling of 
Guggulu in Dola Yantra is carried on until all the 
Guggulu passes into the fluid through the cloth. 


The residue in the bundle is discarded. The 
fluid is filtered and again boiled till it forms a mass. 
This mass is dried in sun light and then pounded 
with a pestle in a stone mortar, adding ghee in 
small quantities till it becomes waxy.

18. JAIPALA SODHANA 
(Rasamrta, Parisista 8; Page 146.) 


जेपालं निस्तुषं कृत्वा दुग्धे दोलायुतं पचेत्‌ । 
अन्तर्जिंह्वा परित्यज्य निम्बुनीरेण भावयेत्‌ 
एव शुद्धं तु जेपालं रसयोगेषु योजयेत्‌ 

(रसामृत, परिशिष्ट 8, पृष्ठ 146) 
(Sd.) 


Q.S. for svedana 
Q.S. for bhavana 


1. Jaipala 1 part 
2. Dugdha (go dugdha) 


3. Nimbt-nira 


Method 

The testa of Jaipala is removed and the seeds 
are subjected to svedana in dola yantra with 
godugdha for three hours. The embryo of the seed 
is removed. The cotyledons dried and powdered. 
Bhavana with nimbu svarasa is given for three 
days, then it is dried in sun and used.

Method 
Small pieces of tankana are placed in a vessel 
on fire and collected when dehydrated.

Visesa sodhana 
(Ayurvedaprakasa, Adhyaya 3; 118-118%.) 


विशेष शोधन 
स्नुहयर्कक्षीरसिन्धूत्थेस्ताम्रपत्राणि लेपयेत्‌ । 
अग्नौ प्रताप्य निर्गुण्डीरसे संसेचयेत्त्रिश 1118 || 
स्नुहयर्कक्षीरसेकैर्वा शुल्वशुद्धिः प्रजायते || 
(आयुर्वेदप्रकाश, अध्याय 3, 118-118 %) 


Qs. 


Tamra patra 
Snuhi ksira (L.) Q.S. (for lepana) 
Arka ksira (L.) Q.S. (for lepana) 
Sindhittha (saindhava) QS. (for lepana) 
Nirgundi rasa (Lf.)Q.S. (for lepana) 


Method of visesa sodhana 

Saindhava is mixed with snuhi ksira and arka 
ksira to a paste which is smeared on tamra patra 
‘and allowed to dry. Thereafter, tamra patra is heated 
and immersed in nirgundi svarasa: This process is 
repeated 3 times. Another process is that tamra 
patra is heated and while on the oven snuhi ksira 
and arka ksira are dropped over it in small quanti- 
ties and allowed to evaporate.

22. DHATTURA SODHANA 
(Rasamrta, Parisista 8; Page 146.) 


धत्तूर बीजं गोमूत्रे चतुर्यामोषित पुनः 

गव्यो पयसि दोलायां यामेक पाचितं ततः || 

कण्डितं निस्तुषं कृत्वा शुद्ध योगेषु योजयेत्‌ । 

(रसामृत, परिशिष्ट 8; पृष्ठ 146) 

1. Dhatttira-bija (Sd.) 1 part 

2. Go-mitra Q:S. 

3. Gavya payas (godugdha) Q.S. for 
svedana 


Method 

Dhattiira seeds are soaked in go-miitra for 12 
hours. Then they are washed with water and 
subjected to svedana in a dola yantra containing 
go-dugdha for 3 hours. The seeds are used after 


removing testa. 


23. NAVASARA SODHANA 
(Rasatarangini, Taranga 14; 3-4.) 


नवसारन्तु सलिले त्रिगुणे द्रावयेद्धिषक्‌ 


वस्त्रपूतं ततः कृत्वा भाजने स्थापयेत्ततः. || 3 ॥। 
चुल्लिकायां निधायाथ पचेत्तीत्राग्निना भृशम्‌ । 
जले शुष्के तलस्थञ्च नृसार विमलं हरेत्‌ ।। 4 II 
(रसतरगिणी, तरग 14, 3-4)

24. NAGA SODHANA 


sodhana 
Sarngadharasamhita Madhyamakhanda 
Adhyaya 11; 2-2%) 
शोधन 
निषिञ्चेत्तप्ततप्तानि तैले तक्रे च काञ्जिके 112 11 
गोमूत्रे च कुलत्थानां कषाये च त्रिधा त्रिधा । 
(शा ई.धरसहिता, मध्यम खण्ड, अध्याय 11. 2-2 1/2) 


1. Naga Q:S. 

2. Taila (tila) (Sd.) Q.S. (for nirvapana) 
3. Takra Q.S. (for nirvapana) 
4. Gomitra Q.S. (for nirvapana) 
5. Kanjika Q.S. (for nirvapana) 
6. Kulattha-kasaya (Sd.) Q.S. (for nirvapana) 


Method of sodhana 

The naga is melted in an iron pan. It is poured 
into an iron pot containing taila, etc. in sufficient 
quantity. The pot should be covered with a heavy 
stone having a hole in the centre and the melted 
naga is poured through this hole. This process is 
to be repeated 3 times in each of the five drugs.

26. BHALLATAKA SODHANA 
(Rasamrta, Parisista 8; Page 147) 


भल्लातकान्‌ सुपक्वास्तु गोमूत्रे दिनसप्तकम्‌ | 
तथा च गव्यपयसि वासयेदिन सप्तकम्‌ || 


ततो घृष्ट्वेष्टिकाचूर्णैः क्षालयित्वाऽम्भसा पुनः | 
संशोष्य मारूतेनाथ वृन्तं छित्वा प्रयोजयेत्‌ ।। 
(रसामृत, परिशिष्ट 8; पृष्ठ 147) 


1. Bhallataka (Fr.) 1 part 
2. Go-miutra 0.3. for 
vasana 

3. Gavya payas (go dugdha) Q.S. for 
vasana 

4. Istika curna 0.3. for 
gharsana 

5. Ambhas (jala) Q.S. for 
ksalana 


Method 

Bhallataka fruits after removing the attachment 
of thalamus are soaked in gomitra for seven days 
and thereafter in go-dugdha for seven days. The 
seeds are then put into bag containing coarse brick 
powder with which they are rubbed carefully, with 
a view to reduce the oil content. Then the fruits 
are washed with water and dried in air.

38. VACA SODHANA 
(Bhaisajyaratnavali, Vatavyadhyadhikara; 446-7) 


Vaca is boiled in Gomittra and Alambusa 
Kvatha and later in pancapallava kvatha and dried. 
Cleaned with gandhodaka, dried and kept in a pot 
of many small holes, which in turn is kept on a 
big pot containing gandhodaka. And the bigger 
pot is kept on fire. After one hour svedana vaca is 
kept in Gomutra for another one hour. Later 
washed with Sigru tvak kvatha and gandhodaka 
and kept in flowers of madhika and mallika for 
imparting fragrance. Later vaca is powdered and 
given dhupa with rala, kundaru and nakhi and 
stored in glass bottles.

1. Silajatu 2 parts

Method 

Powder of silajatu (stone) vigourously mixed 
with hot water so as to disengage the soluble matter, 
it is allowed to settle and the supernatant 
suspension is decanted and concentrated to thick 
paste or solid amorphus surface. 

The above extracted silajatu or silajatu as such 
available in the market is subjected to further 
Sodhana as under. 

Silajatu is mixed with double the quantity of 
water and half the quantity of Triphala kvatha and 
carefully boiled to dryness.

Method of sodhana 

The svarna maksika is powdered and sieved. 
The powder is placed in an iron pan and nimbuka 
svarasa is added and heated. It is to be stirred 
continuously till the moisture has evaporated. The 
svarma maksika is pink in colour and it takes 2 to 


3 days to complete this process.

57. HINGULOTTHA PARADA SODHANA 
(Rasamrta, Adhyaya 1; 16-17) 


अथवा दरदाकृष्टं स्विन्नं लवणाम्बुभिस्तु दोलायाम्‌ । 


रसमादाय यथेच्छ कर्तव्यस्तेन भेषजो योगः ।116 II 
निम्बूरसेन संपिष्टत्‌ प्रहर दरदाटूढम्‌ 
तिर्यक्पातनयन्त्रेण संग्राह्यो निर्मलो रसः 1117 || 
(रसामृत, अध्याय 1; 16-17.) 
1. Hingula 1 part 
2. Nimbirasa (Fr.) Q.S. for 
mardana 


Method 

Hingula should be rubbed well in a khalva with 
lime juice. Small cakrikas are prepared dried and 
kept in tiryakpatana yantra and heated. Heating is 
to be stopped when the whole quantity of parada 
is collected. 


ASTASAMSKARA OF PARADA 


In the ancient times mercury (parada) obtained 
in a crude form used to be highly contaminated 
with several impurities and therefore Astasamskara 
of mercury was prescribed for its purification and 
potentisation. However, in modern times mercury 
can be recovered from Hingula by sublimation or 
distillation. This mercury is sufficiently pure for 
medicinal use. 

Alternatively the mercury available in the me- 
tallic form should be subjected to distillation three 
times in an iron retort (Tiryak patana yantra). The 
final distillate is fairly pure for medicinal use. 

Astasamskarita parada is also used in the 
preparation of medicines. 


1. SVEDANA 
(Rasahrdayatantra, Avabodha 2; 3) 


आसुरिपटुकटुकत्रयचित्रार्द्रकमूलकेः कलाशैश्च | 
सूतस्य काञ्जिकेन त्रिदिनं मृदुवहिनिना स्वेदयेत्‌ 113 II 
(रसहदयतत्र, अवबोध 2; 3) 


1. Stita (parada) 1 part 
2. Asuri (rajika) (Sd.) 1/16 part 
3. Patu (saindhava lavana) 1/16 part 
4. Sunthi (Rz.) 1/16 part 
5. Marica (Fr.) 1/16 part 
6. Pippali (Fr.) 1/16 part 


373 


7. Citraka-mitla (Rt.) 1/16 part 
8. Ardraka (Rz.) 1/16 part 
9. Miulaka (Rt. Tr.) 1/16 part 
10. Kanjika Q.S. for svedana 
Method 


Pottali is prepared by taking four fold cloth, 
Bhurja patra or kadali patra is to be placed on this 
four fold cloth, then besmear the leaf with pre- 
pared kalka, on which Parada is gently placed. 
The remaining kalka is put on parada, and then 
the pottali is tied by a string immersed in dola yantra 
containing Kanjika, then heated for three days. 

After three days heating, kalka and parada 
taken out from pottali, washed with warm water 
carefully and parada collected for other samskara 
i.e.mardana. 


2. MARDANA 
(Rasahrdayatantra, Avabodha 2;4.) 


गुड दग्धोर्णालवणेर्मन्दिर धूमेष्टकासुरी alec: | 


रसषोडशामानेः सकाजिञ्कैर्मर्दनं त्रिदिनम्‌ ।14।| 
(रसहदयतत्र, अवबोध 2, 4) 

1. Rasa (parada) { part 
2. Guda 1/16 part 
3. Dagdhorna 1/16 part 
4. Lavana (saindhava lavana) 1/16 part 
5. Mandira dhima (grha dhiima) 1/16 part 
6. Istika (पा) 1/16 part 
7. Asuri (rajika) 1/16 part 
8. Kanjika Q.S. for mardana 
Method 


Ingredients 2 to 7 are mixed with parada. 
Kanjika in required quantity is added and rubbed 
well for three days in a Khalva. Thereafter, the 
mixture of parada and kalka dravyas are washed 
with warm water or warm kafjika and is squeezed 
through a thick cloth and the mercury is separated. 


3. MURCCHANA 
(Rasamrta, Adhyaya 1; 10.) 
गृहकन्या हरति मलं, त्रिफलाऽग्नि चित्रकश्च विषम्‌ । 


तस्मादेभिमिन्रर्वारान्‌ समूरच्छयेत्‌ सप्त 1110 || 
(रसामृत, अध्याय 1; 10.) 


1. Parada 1 part 
2. Grhakanya (kumari) (Lf.) 1/16 part 
3. Haritaki (P.) 1/16 part 
4. Bibhitaka (P.) 1/16 part 
5. Amalaki (P.) 1/16 part 
6. Citraka (Rt.) 1/16 part 
Method 


Ingredients 2 to 6 are mixed with parada and 
rubbed well in khalva for three days or till 
murcchana stage of parada is reached. Then this 
mercury is ready for subjecting to utthapana 
samskara. 


4. UTTHAPANA 
(Rasahrdayatantra, Avabodha 2; 7.) 


अमुना विरेचनेन हि सुविशुद्धो नागवंगपरिमुक्तः | 
सूतः पातनयन्त्रे समुत्थितः काजिकक्वाथात्‌ 117 I | 
(रसहदयतत्र, अवबोध 2, 7.) 


1. Parada 1 part 
2. Kanjika Q.S. for washing 
Method 


Murcchita parada is subjected to the process 
of tiryakpatana (distillation). Thereafter the distil- 
late (mercury) is to be washed with (warm) kanjika 


5. PATANA 
Patana is of three types, viz. Urdhvapatana 
Adhahpatana and Tiryakpatana. 
Details of their procedure is as below. 


A. URDHVAPATANA 
(AyurvedaprakasSa, Adhyaya 1; 68-73.) 


भागास्त्रयो रसस्यार्कचूर्णस्येकोऽथ निम्बुकैः 1168 ||

(आयुर्वेद प्रकाश, अध्याय 1; 68-73) 
1. २२३५३८०० (parada) 3 parts 
2. Ravi (tamra) 1 part 
3. Jambira (nimbi) rasa (Fr.) QS. for 
mardana. 


Method 

Ravi (tamra) clirna is added to parada and 
rubbed well in a khalva with nimbii svarasa. This 
is to be pasted in the lower pot of damaru yantra 
and heated for three hours. The parada is collected 
from the inside of upper pot, which is kept cool, 
with a wet cloth-pad. 

B. ADHAH PATANA 
(Ayurvedaprakasa, Adhyaya 1; 75-77.) 


त्रिफला Rryafara लवणासुरिकायुतेः | 


नष्टपिष्ट रसं कृत्वा लैपयेदर्ध्वभाण्डके 1/5

8. Sigru-tvak (St.Bk.) 1/16 part 
9. Nimbi-rasa (Fr.) Q.S. for mardana. 
Method 


Mercury is triturated with ingredients 2 to 8 in 
a Khalva using lemon juice as a macerating agent 
till it reaches extremely fine paste form. From this 
paste, mercury should be collected through Adhah 
patana yantra.

(Ayurveda prakaSa, Adhyaya 1; 79-81%) 


घटे रसं विनिक्षिप्य सजलं घटमन्यकम्‌

days inside soil.
